### [1] [RISE: Adaptive Imagination for World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20430)
*Hongbo Lu,Liang Yao,Chenghao He,Hao Han,Fan Liu,Wenlong Liao,Tao He,Pai Peng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Adaptive imagination in World Action Models through selective rollout (RISE) with a latent evaluator and rollout gate, plus a CounterDrive counterfactual dataset; improves planning efficiency and generalizes across WAM architectures, validated on NAVSIM/nuScenes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fixed imagination budgets in world-model-based planning waste compute and can be unsafe. There is a need for adaptive, risk-aware, and data-enriched planning that selectively imagines future trajectories.

Method: RISE introduces sequential Roll/Stop decisions driven by a Latent Evaluator (risk plus potential gain) and a Rollout Gate (benefit vs compute cost). It leverages CounterDrive, a diverse counterfactual dataset with expert annotations, to supervise world-model dynamics; designed to plug into existing WAM architectures.

Result: RISE achieves the best overall planning performance with reduced unnecessary rollout in NAVSIM and nuScenes; transfer experiments show plug-in generality across WAMs; CounterDrive enriches safety-critical supervision.

Conclusion: Adaptive, selective imagination improves planning efficiency and effectiveness for WAMs, aided by counterfactual data that enhances risk-aware dynamics modeling and provides reusable safety annotations.

Abstract: World Action Models (WAMs) improve planning by incorporating future world evolution into action generation, yet existing methods allocate a fixed imagination budget to every scene. We propose RISE (\textbf{R}efining \textbf{I}magination through \textbf{SE}lective Rollout), a system-level adaptive imagination framework that makes sequential \textsc{Roll}/\textsc{Stop} decisions according to the expected planning benefit of continued rollout. At each step, a Latent Evaluator estimates the risk revealed by the current prefix and how much planning could improve if imagination continues, while a Rollout Gate weighs this expected benefit against additional computation cost. Since factual driving logs expose only one realized future, we further construct \textbf{CounterDrive}, a counterfactual dataset with diverse outcomes and risk levels, to enrich future dynamics and provide localized risk supervision. Each retained sample undergoes expert verification and annotation of trajectory validity, incident onset, and causal category, providing a reusable resource for safety-critical world-modeling research. Experiments on NAVSIM and nuScenes show that RISE achieves the best overall planning performance while reducing unnecessary rollout, with additional transfer results supporting its plug-in generality across WAM architectures.

</details>


### [2] [Aggregating Visual Information with Optimal Transport for VideoLM Token Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20473)
*Wenti Yin,Xiaotian Han,Junyuan Shang,Yuchen Ding,Shuohuan Wang,Dianhai Yu,Changxin Gao,Nong Sang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AVIOT compresses video tokens by solving an optimal-transport problem that maps dense frame observations to a compact target representation, yielding a source-to-target coupling that defines how compressed content is formed. It adapts transport along task and spatial axes, conditioning on questions to allocate capacity, and computes region-specific temporal plans with adaptive fusion across regions. It matches or exceeds uncompressed baselines across compression ratios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce the visual-token burden for video-language models while preserving task-relevant information dispersed across frames, addressing redundancy in dense video token sequences.

Method: Represent video observations as a dense empirical measure per frame and transport them onto a compact target measure using optimal transport. The source-to-target coupling induces a distribution over source observations for each target support. Question conditioning modulates transport costs and per-segment support allocation. At multiple spatial scales, compute region-specific temporal transport plans and adaptively fuse representations from different moments.

Result:  AVIOT achieves performance that matches or surpasses the uncompressed baseline on several video-understanding benchmarks across various compression ratios, maintaining strong performance at higher compression.

Conclusion: OT-based aggregating and spatial-temporal adaptation enable effective, flexible video token compression that preserves task-relevant information while reducing token burden.

Abstract: Video language models process videos as dense visual-token sequences with substantial representational redundancy. Compressing these sequences is therefore essential for reducing the visual-token burden on language-model decoding. The central challenge is to preserve visual information dispersed across frames under such compression. To this end, we introduce Aggregating Visual Information with Optimal Transport (AVIOT), which casts video token compression as transporting a dense empirical measure of frame observations onto a compact target measure. The resulting source-to-target coupling induces a distribution over source observations for each target support, directly specifying how the compressed video representation is constructed. We further adapt this construction along task and spatial axes. Question conditioning modulates the transport cost between source frames and target supports, while influencing how many supports are allocated to each temporal segment, thereby directing representation capacity toward question-relevant content. At multiple spatial granularities, AVIOT computes region-specific temporal transport plans and adaptively fuses the representations they yield, allowing different regions within the same compact representation to draw from different moments. Evaluations across varying compression ratios show that AVIOT matches or outperforms the uncompressed baseline on multiple video-understanding benchmarks while retaining strong performance at higher compression ratios.

</details>


### [3] [Annotations as Rollouts: Efficient and Scalable Reinforcement Learning for Video MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20492)
*Yunheng Li,Guohong Mu,Hao Li,Shengsheng Qian,Dingwen Zhang,Qibin Hou,Ming-Ming Cheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OraRL introduces a decoupled advantage estimator for RL post-training of video multimodal LLMs, leveraging oracle rollouts as direct optimization targets with sign-balanced pruning to improve sample efficiency and scalability, achieving faster inference and stronger benchmarks than prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Improve sample efficiency and scalability of reinforcement-learning post-training for video MLLMs by exploiting annotation-derived oracle rollouts as direct optimization targets while mitigating reward-baseline misalignment (advantage inversion).

Method: Decoupled advantage estimator: policy rollouts set an oracle-free baseline; the oracle-policy gap modulates a directional gain and a separate detached oracle advantage. Sign-balanced pruning retains only the oracle and the strongest rollouts per sign to enhance efficiency. Shows significant speedups and scalability across model sizes and datasets.

Result: OraRL achieves 2.2x step-time vs SFT and under 4.9x of GRPO with CoT; scales from 0.8B to 9B parameters and up to 100k prompts; Video-ORA-9B without CoT decodes in 130 ms (vs 4,780 ms). On benchmarks, improves temporal mIoU (62.5→66.0), AO (73.0→78.2), segmentation (64.3→70.4), VSI-Bench (73.1 vs 55.0/55.1 for baselines).

Conclusion: OraRL delivers efficient, scalable RL post-training for video MLLMs by leveraging oracle annotations via a decoupled advantage estimator and sign-balanced pruning, achieving notable performance gains and faster inference relative to state-of-the-art methods.

Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have become a prevailing paradigm for unified video perception. However, post-training on large multi-task datasets remains challenging, as existing reinforcement learning methods sample on-policy groups with few high-quality rollouts even with costly chain-of-thought (CoT) generation. In this paper, we study the sample efficiency and scalability of RL post-training for video MLLMs and introduce OraRL. We identify an overlooked role for annotations: Beyond scoring rollouts, each can enter its on-policy group as an oracle rollout, a direct positive optimization target. Direct oracle integration, however, is nontrivial: a high-reward oracle raises the group baseline and inverts otherwise positive policy advantages, a failure we term advantage inversion. At the core of OraRL is a decoupled advantage estimator: policy rollouts determine an oracle-free baseline, while the oracle-policy gap modulates both a directional gain and a separate detached oracle advantage. Sign-balanced pruning improves efficiency: by retaining only the oracle and the strongest rollouts of each sign, OraRL requires just 2.2x the step time of SFT, less than half the 4.9x required by GRPO with CoT. OraRL scales with model size and data, surpassing its backbone from 0.8B to 9B and GRPO up to 100k prompts. Without chain-of-thought, Video-ORA-9B decodes in 130 ms instead of 4,780 ms. Compared with the respective prior best models, it raises temporal mIoU from 62.5 to 66.0, tracking AO from 73.0 to 78.2, segmentation from 64.3 to 70.4, and the three-benchmark spatial-intelligence macro average from 51.0 to 56.1; on VSI-Bench, it scores 73.1 against 55.0 for GPT-5 and 55.1 for Gemini-3-Pro.

</details>


### [4] [DiffVC-ONE: Diffusion-based Generative Video Compression with One-Step Video Diffusion Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20515)
*Wenzhuo Ma,Zhenzhong Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DiffVC-ONE is a diffusion-based video compression framework that combines a Unified Unidirectional Latent Compressor, a Video DiT-based One-Step Diffusion Enhancer, and a Hybrid Condition Generator to deliver high perceptual quality and temporal consistency at low inference cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Generative video compression struggles to simultaneously achieve high temporal consistency, rich perceptual quality, and low inference cost at low bitrates; the paper proposes a diffusion-based solution to address these trade-offs.

Method: Proposes three components: (1) Unified Unidirectional Latent Compressor (shared model to compress latent slices); (2) Video DiT-based One-Step Diffusion Enhancer (uses latent slices as content anchors for single-step spatio-temporal enhancement across a GOP); (3) Hybrid Condition Generator (extracts structural, strength, semantic conditions from reconstructed content and quantization info to guide diffusion).

Result: Extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks show state-of-the-art perceptual quality and temporal consistency with low inference cost.

Conclusion: DiffVC-ONE delivers improved perceptual video quality and temporal stability at efficient inference by integrating unified latent compression, one-step diffusion enhancement, and content-aware condition guidance.

Abstract: Generative video compression can recover rich visual details at low bitrates, but simultaneously achieving high temporal consistency and low inference cost remains challenging. To address this issue, we propose DiffVC-ONE, a diffusion-based generative video compression framework built on a one-step Video Diffusion Transformer. First, we introduce a Unified Unidirectional Latent Compressor that uses a shared model to efficiently and uniformly compress compact latent slices. We then develop a Video DiT-based One-Step Diffusion Enhancer that uses the reconstructed latent slices as content anchors and performs single-step spatio-temporal perceptual enhancement over an entire group of pictures. Finally, a Hybrid Condition Generator extracts structural, strength, and semantic conditions from the reconstructed content and quantization information. These conditions preserve faithful regions, control the degree of generative enhancement, and supplement content-aware perceptual details during one-step diffusion enhancement. Extensive experiments on multiple standard benchmarks demonstrate that DiffVC-ONE achieves state-of-the-art perceptual quality and temporal consistency with low inference cost.

</details>


### [5] [Grounded-Exo2Ego: Structured Semantic Grounding for Robust Exocentric-to-Egocentric Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20534)
*Shengze Wang,Michael Stengel,Tianye Li,Seonwook Park,Amrita Mazumdar,Koki Nagano,Alex Trevithick,Shalini De Mello*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A dual-branch diffusion framework for exo-to-ego video generation combining geometry-driven 3D reconstruction conditioning with a semantic grounding module, plus camera re-localization and a synthetic data engine; achieves large gains on EgoExo4D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Exo-to-ego generation is challenging due to extreme viewpoint changes and large unobservable regions; standard geometry conditioning is unreliable, necessitating semantic grounding and robust calibration, aided by automated synthetic data.

Method: A dual-branch video diffusion model: a geometric anchoring branch conditionally renders from a 3D reconstruction; a semantic grounding branch leverages object-level context to synthesize difficult regions beyond geometry. A camera re-localization algorithm mitigates camera-reconstruction misalignment. An automated synthetic data engine generates and renders rigged 3D characters in procedurally generated environments.

Result: The approach outperforms recent state-of-the-art methods by large margins on EgoExo4D across metrics; ablations validate the contribution of both architectural design and data generation components.

Conclusion: Combining geometry-based conditioning with semantic grounding, plus robust camera localization and automated synthetic data, yields significant improvements for exo-to-ego video generation, demonstrating the value of integrating semantic context and calibration with geometry in this task.

Abstract: Generating egocentric video from a single exocentric video is an emerging and important topic for AR/VR and physical AI. Compared with conventional novel view synthesis, exo-to-ego generation is a significantly harder task because the standard geometric conditioning becomes highly unreliable under extreme view changes and large unobservable regions. We present Grounded-Exo2Ego, a principled framework that addresses these challenges at both the architectural and data levels. Architecturally, Grounded-Exo2Ego is a dual-branch video diffusion model that couples a geometric anchoring branch, which conditions the generation on the rendering of a 3D reconstruction, with a novel semantic grounding branch, which goes beyond the prevailing geometry-based approach and improves quality by synthesizing challenging regions based on object-level context. Additionally, we found that the overlooked issue of camera-reconstruction misalignment severely undermines exo-to-ego learning. We thus introduce a camera re-localization algorithm that resolves this issue and substantially improves quality across all metrics. We further develop a fully automated synthetic data engine that generates and renders rigged 3D characters in procedurally generated environments. Evaluation on the challenging EgoExo4D dataset shows that our method outperforms recent state-of-the-art approaches by large margins across all metrics. Detailed ablations validate improvements from each of our contributions at both the data and architectural level.

</details>


### [6] [Keep Your Friends Close, and the Right Neighbours Closer: Disaster-Conditioned Kernel-Regularized Graph Attention for Building Damage Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20548)
*Fuad Hasan,Chul Min Yeum*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A geo-attention model with a disaster-type conditioned multi-scale spatial kernel and residual decorrelation loss improves spatial-context use for disaster damage classification, yielding higher macro-F1 and lower residual spatial autocorrelation, and generalizes better across events and to Ida-BD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Disaster damage exhibits strong spatial clustering, but many pipelines rely on local appearance cues and use uniform smoothing; there is a need for adaptive, event-specific spatial reasoning to improve robustness and transferability across different disaster types.

Method: Represent each building with a pre/post combined (PPC) patch from polygons and model spatial context with GPS-derived building graphs. Introduce a disaster-type-conditioned graph-based multi-scale spatial kernel in attention to adapt the neighborhood scale per disaster type. Add a residual de-correlation loss to penalize positive Moran's I in prediction residuals. Evaluate with leave-one-event-out (LOEO) on xBD and cross-dataset transfer from xBD to Ida-BD.

Result: The approach improves macro-F1 and substantially reduces residual spatial autocorrelation under zero-shot event shift, indicating better use of spatial context rather than naive smoothing and enabling more reliable transfer to unseen events within known disaster types.

Conclusion: Adaptive, disaster-type-aware spatial reasoning coupled with residual decorrelation yields more reliable damage classification with better generalization across events and datasets, addressing the risk of over-smoothing boundaries while leveraging spatial context.

Abstract: Disaster damage is spatial: buildings rarely fail in isolation. Yet using spatial context for damage classification remains surprisingly underexplored, and many pipelines still rely primarily on per-building appearance cues even when the dominant uncertainty is spatially structured. Complicating matters, the right neighbourhood is not the same across events. Floods, hurricanes, and wildfires can exhibit very different clustering behaviour, making spatial reasoning valuable but easy to misuse - naive context aggregation can improve visual coherence while oversmoothing boundaries or propagating structured errors. We study this tension on xBD (the dataset used in the xView2 challenge) in a controlled post-localization, classification-only setup: each building is represented by a pre/post combined (PPC) patch cropped from the provided polygons, and spatial context is modelled with GPS-derived building graphs. Our approach keeps local evidence "close" by preserving strong spatial relationships in disaster damage patterns, while bringing only the right neighbours "closer" through a disaster-type-conditioned graph model that injects a learnable multi-scale spatial kernel prior into attention, allowing the effective neighbourhood scale to adapt across disaster types rather than being learned as a single global smoothing rule. To discourage coherence-by-smoothing, we add a residual de-correlation loss that penalizes positive Moran's~I in prediction residuals. We evaluate the method under event and dataset shift with a leave-one-event-out (LOEO) protocol on xBD and cross-dataset transfer from xBD to Ida-BD. The model improves macro-F1 and substantially reduces residual spatial autocorrelation under zero-shot event shift, indicating better use of spatial context rather than naive smoothing and enabling more reliable transfer to unseen events within known disaster types.

</details>


### [7] [Learning Prostate Anatomy at Test Time for Cancer Detection in Micro-Ultrasound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20557)
*Obed Korshie Dzikunu,Mohammad Mahdi Abootorabi,Mohamed Harmanani,Paul F. R. Wilson,Emma Willis,Ferdinand Luger,Adam Kinnaird,Brian Wodlinger,Parvin Mousavi,Purang Abolmaesumi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ANT is a test-time adaptation method for prostate cancer detection that uses segmentation-guided training to align the encoder's representations with target-domain prostate anatomy, via pseudo-masks from a frozen segmentation network, improving detection performance under cross-center domain shift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Domain shift across clinical centers with different imaging hardware/protocols degrades deep learning cancer detection. Existing TTA methods address appearance but ignore target anatomy; aligning representations to anatomy at test time could reduce feature drift while preserving cancer-discriminative signals.

Method: At test time, ANT adapts a pretrained cancer-detection encoder to the target domain by solving an auxiliary prostate segmentation task. This task is supervised by pseudo-masks generated by a frozen pretrained segmentation network, guiding the encoder to align with target-domain prostate anatomy. Training used 693 patients from an older-generation micro-ultrasound scanner in multicenter data; evaluation used 118 patients from newer-generation systems across two centers, under a leave-one-center-out protocol. Performance is compared to no adaptation and other TTA baselines.

Result: ANT yields mean AUC gains of 2.9% (biopsy-core level) and 3.6% (patient level) over no adaptation, and outperforms standard TTA baselines.

Conclusion: Segmentation-guided test-time adaptation effectively mitigates domain-specific feature drift by enforcing anatomical-consistent representations in the target domain, preserving cancer-discriminative information. Code is publicly available at the provided GitHub link.

Abstract: Domain shift across clinical centers using different imaging hardware or acquisition protocols remains a fundamental barrier to deploying deep learning models for prostate cancer (PCa) detection. Existing test-time adaptation (TTA) methods address distribution shift through entropy minimization or augmentation-based self-supervision, correcting for statistical differences in image appearance but ignoring the anatomical structure of the target domain. We propose ANT, a segmentation-guided TTA framework that adapts a pretrained cancer detection encoder to the target domain by solving an auxiliary prostate segmentation task at test time, supervised by pseudo-masks from a frozen pretrained segmentation network. By aligning encoder representations to prostate anatomy in the target domain, ANT corrects domain-specific feature drift while preserving cancer-discriminative structure. The model was trained on 693 patients imaged with an earlier-generation micro-ultrasound scanner in a multi-center clinical trial, and evaluated on 118 patients acquired with a newer-generation system across two centers in another clinical trial. Under a leave-one-center-out protocol with identical evaluation conditions across all methods, ANT improves mean AUC by 2.9% and 3.6% at the biopsy-core and patient levels, respectively, over no adaptation, outperforming TTA baselines. Code is available at: https://github.com/ObedDzik/ant.git.

</details>


### [8] [Zero-Shot Color Image Manipulation Localization via Noise Residual Artifact Pattern Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20558)
*Edgar Gonzalez-Fernandez*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Zero-shot blind image manipulation localization using a single image’s noise residual to estimate a reference artifact pattern, avoiding training or device enrollment and not assuming fixed filters or block patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome limitations of passive methods that rely on the green Bayer channel, require training data, or device enrollment; leverage full color residuals and correlation to detect forgeries without prior exposure to devices.

Method: A pipeline that (1) estimates a reference artifact pattern from the image’s noise residual, without fixed color layout or block period; (2) uses a denoiser selection criterion based on acquired-to-interpolated noise variance; (3) performs block-level correlation against the estimated reference pattern; (4) applies a two-component Gaussian Mixture Model to produce a pixel-level tampering probability map; (5) includes ablation studies on denoiser choice and block size.

Result: The approach is competitive with state-of-the-art passive methods, showing effective zero-shot localization without training data or device enrollment, and it yields a pixel-level tampering map through the proposed scoring stages.

Conclusion: A training-free, single-image localization framework that exploits color-channel residuals and a data-driven denoiser decision, enabling robust tampering localization without fixed filters or block configurations and offering competitive performance against existing passive methods.

Abstract: Digital cameras embed device-specific artifacts into every acquired image through demosaicing, in-camera post-processing, and lossy compression. These traces constitute a forensic signal that can be exploited to assess image authenticity. Existing passive methods rely predominantly on the green channel of the Bayer residual, discarding the correlated information available in the remaining color channels and typically requiring training data or device enrollment. This work proposes a zero-shot, training-free blind image manipulation localization pipeline that estimates a reference artifact pattern directly from the noise residual of a single suspect image, without assuming a fixed filter configuration, color layout, or block period. The pipeline incorporates a principled denoiser selection criterion based on the acquired-to-interpolated noise variance ratio, a block-level correlation analysis against the estimated reference pattern, and a two-component Gaussian Mixture Model scoring stage that produces a pixel-level tampering probability map. An ablation study evaluates the impact of denoiser choice and block size on localization accuracy, and comparisons against state-of-the-art passive methods demonstrate the competitiveness of the proposed zero-shot approach.

</details>


### [9] [Aggregate, Don't Adapt: Subject-Level Posterior Aggregation and Transductive Calibration for Cross-Site Parkinsonian Gait Severity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20587)
*Junlong Shen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: State-of-the-art 0.6945 macro-F1 on MoCha 2026 Parkinsonian Gait using a frozen SMPL encoder and subject-level aggregation; gains come from three calibration/data-processing steps, not encoder fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Predict MDS-UPDRS gait severity from canonicalized SMPL motion recorded at clinical sites unseen during training, enabling robust, cross-site gait assessment in Parkinson's disease.

Method: Frozen public motion encoder with a single 4x512 linear layer. Three key stages: (1) reproducing the benchmark's exact head recipe, (2) averaging per-walk posteriors within subject groups, and (3) label-free transductive calibration of feature mean and decision threshold. Ablations show fine-tuning the encoder is detrimental; ten alternative encoders perform worse; leave-two-cohort-out CV is anti-correlated with test score.

Result: 0.6945 macro-F1 on the hidden test, ranking first among 58 entries; runner-up 0.5807; organizer baseline 0.4289.

Conclusion: Subject-level aggregation defines the performance ceiling; most margin derives from three non-model steps rather than encoder gains; the authors disclose a full negative record and note cross-validation did not predict test outcomes.

Abstract: We describe the winning entry to the MoCha 2026 Benchmark and Challenge on Parkinsonian Gait, which predicts MDS-UPDRS gait severity from canonicalized SMPL motion recorded at clinical sites unseen during training. The system reaches 0.6945 macro-F1 on the hidden test and ranked first of 58 entries, ahead of the runner-up at 0.5807 and the organizers' baseline at 0.4289, on a frozen public motion encoder with a single $4\times512$ linear layer. Nearly all of the margin comes from three stages usually treated as bookkeeping: reproducing the reference benchmark's exact head recipe, averaging per-walk posteriors within the subject grouping the organizers ship, and a label-free transductive calibration of the feature mean and the decision operating point. Fine-tuning the encoder lost in four distinct forms, and ten alternative encoders were worse. Every ablation number is a paid read on the hidden test, because our own leave-two-cohort-out cross-validation proved anti-correlated with the deciding score over eleven configurations. We give the negative record in full, and identify our largest gain, subject-level aggregation, as the binding ceiling on this benchmark.

</details>


### [10] [A Dataset-Centric Benchmark of Deep Learning Methods for Grape Leaf Disease Classification and Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20608)
*Petar Canoski,Vlatko Spasev,Ivica Dimitrovski,Ivan Kitanovski,Petre Lameski*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A dataset-centric benchmark evaluating deep learning for grape leaf disease classification and detection across public datasets; reveals dataset biases, cross-dataset generalization gaps, especially in detection; calls for provenance, realistic evaluation, annotation compatibility, external validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address reliance on a few controlled datasets and to examine how dataset provenance, annotation schemes, and acquisition conditions affect grape leaf disease recognition, enabling more robust, transferable models.

Method: Survey publicly available grape leaf datasets by disease categories, annotation types, acquisition conditions, image characteristics, class distributions, provenance, and task suitability. Evaluate representative models in three settings: image-level classification, region-level classification, and object detection. Use accuracy for classification and mAP@50 and mAP@50:95 for detection. Conduct cross-dataset transfer experiments to study generalization across datasets with compatible disease categories but differing visual/annotation characteristics.

Result: Classification achieves near-saturated performance on controlled/derivative datasets; heterogeneous datasets are more challenging. Detection performance varies substantially across annotation settings. Cross-dataset transfer performance drops sharply, especially for object detection, indicating that shared disease labels do not imply identical recognition tasks.

Conclusion: Benchmark highlights the importance of dataset provenance, realistic field evaluation, annotation compatibility, and external validation for reliable vineyard disease recognition.

Abstract: Grape leaf disease recognition is important for precision agriculture, enabling early diagnosis, timely intervention, and improved vineyard management. Although deep learning has achieved strong results, many studies rely on few datasets, often acquired under controlled conditions, and may not reflect real vineyard challenges such as complex backgrounds, variable illumination, occlusion, leaf pose, disease severity, and device differences. This paper presents a dataset-centric benchmark of deep learning methods for grape leaf disease classification and detection. We analyze publicly available datasets in terms of disease categories, annotation types, acquisition conditions, image characteristics, class distributions, provenance, and task suitability. Representative models are evaluated in three settings: image-level classification, region-level classification, and object detection. Classification is assessed using accuracy, while detection is evaluated using mAP@50 and mAP@50:95. Cross-dataset experiments further examine transfer between datasets with compatible disease categories but different visual and annotation characteristics. Results show near-saturated classification performance on several controlled or derivative datasets, greater difficulty on heterogeneous datasets, and substantial variation in detection performance across annotation settings. Cross-dataset performance drops sharply, especially for object detection, indicating that shared disease labels do not necessarily define equivalent recognition tasks. The benchmark emphasizes dataset provenance, realistic field evaluation, annotation compatibility, and external validation for reliable vineyard disease recognition.

</details>


### [11] [RECOUNT: Reference-guided Counting with Synthetic Visual Exemplars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20621)
*Adriano D'Alessandro,Ali Mahdavi-Amiri,Ghassan Hamarneh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RECOUNT enables zero-shot counting by converting a single off-scene reference image into a diverse gallery via a diffusion model, decoupling localization from categorization. It plugs into any frozen counter and uses a synthetic-data–trained visual module to compare proposals against target vs. distractors, achieving state-of-the-art zero-shot accuracy on LookAlikes and PairTally with substantial MAE reductions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-guided zero-shot counters excel at localization but struggle with fine-grained or novel categories; few-shot counters require annotations. The aim is accurate, annotation-free zero-shot counting by leveraging a visual reference and synthetic data.

Method: Use an off-scene reference image as a query; employ a diffusion model as a contrastive data engine to generate a diverse exemplar gallery; keep a frozen counting backbone; train a lightweight visual head on synthetic data to match proposals to target vs. distractor galleries; categorization is delegated to this visual module.

Result: Attains best zero-shot accuracy on LookAlikes and PairTally; MAE reductions of 55% and 21% respectively over the strongest prior zero-shot counter.

Conclusion: A plug-and-play framework that combines image-guided counting with diffusion-generated synthetic data, preserving localization while improving discriminative categorization; scalable and annotation-light beyond the initial reference.

Abstract: Text-guided zero-shot object counters excel at spatial localization but categorize poorly on novel or fine-grained classes: natural language is too coarse to fully specify visual identity, so they fail to separate visually similar distractors. Few-shot counters sidestep this with visual exemplars, but require manual annotations on every image. To resolve this dilemma, we introduce RECOUNT, a plug-and-play framework for image-guided zero-shot counting. Rather than specify a category with a text prompt, our key insight is to specify it visually, from a single off-scene reference image. However, we find that a lone reference image provides narrow coverage of a category's appearance and is unreliable across diverse scenes. We therefore repurpose a diffusion model as an automated contrastive data engine that expands the reference into a diverse exemplar gallery, supplying the discriminative detail that text cannot. RECOUNT preserves the class-agnostic proposals of any frozen counter and offloads categorization to a separate visual module (a frozen backbone with a lightweight head trained on this synthetic data) that matches each proposal against the target and distractor galleries. Applied to a frozen counter, RECOUNT attains the best zero-shot accuracy on both benchmarks, cutting counting error (MAE) by 55% on LookAlikes and 21% on PairTally relative to the strongest prior zero-shot counter.

</details>


### [12] [MV2GF: Multi-view Pedestrian Detection with a Visual Geometric Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20639)
*Taiga Yamane,Satoshi Suzuki,Ryo Masumura,Shota Orihashi,Tomohiro Tanaka,Mana Ihori,Naoki Makishima*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MV2GF uses a visual geometric foundation model to enhance multi-view pedestrian detection, fusing general geometric features with task-specific ones and using 3D pointmaps to map pixels to 3D space, improving generalization to unseen camera configurations and reducing reliance on training distortion patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MVPD methods struggle to generalize to unseen camera configurations due to difficulties in capturing accurate cross-view geometry and sensitivity to distortion patterns learned during training.

Method: Introduce MV2GF, leveraging a visual geometric foundation model to extract general geometric features and fuse them with task-specific features. Project each image pixel to a 3D location using 3D pointmaps predicted by the foundation model, thereby reducing dependence on distortion patterns.

Result: Experiments demonstrate that MV2GF generalizes better than existing MVPD methods, validating the benefit of incorporating a visual geometric foundation model for MVPD.

Conclusion: A visual geometric foundation model improves cross-view geometry capture and reduces distortion-pattern bias in MVPD, enabling more robust detection in unseen camera configurations.

Abstract: Multi-View Pedestrian Detection (MVPD) aims to detect pedestrians in the form of a bird's eye view map from multi-view images. Recent MVPD methods adopt a unified framework that projects 2D image features into a 3D world space and aggregates them into a single feature. Although they are effective, they struggle to generalize to unseen camera configurations during training due to two main issues. First, they are difficult to capture accurate visual geometry across views in unseen camera configurations. Second, they make detection models highly dependent on distortion patterns during training arising from their image feature projection. To address these, we leverage a visual geometric foundation model and propose MV2GF. This foundation model has exhibited strong generalization in capturing visual geometry across views and predicting accurate 3D attributes in diverse camera configurations. MV2GF fuses task-specific features with general-purpose geometric features extracted by the foundation model to effectively capture the visual geometry even in unseen camera configurations. Furthermore, MV2GF projects each pixel in the image features to an appropriate 3D location using 3D pointmaps predicted by the foundation model, preventing the detection model from depending on distortion patterns during training. Our experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of leveraging a visual geometric foundation model for MVPD and that MV2GF generalizes better than existing methods.

</details>


### [13] [Lift, Associate, and Fuse: A Decision-Centric Framework for 2D-to-3D Foundation Model Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20659)
*Wentao Sun,Yiping Chen,John S. Zelek,Jonathan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LAF: a five-operator (Generate, Associate, Reconcile, Fuse, Persist/Query) decision-centric framework to transfer 2D foundation-model predictions into 3D segmentation; defines a persistent carrier contract and audits 161 systems, revealing four recurring properties and enabling representation-neutral system comparison and diagnosis of irreversible failures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address confusion in how 2D-to-3D transfers preserve coherence across views; need explicit decision points and a stable persistent carrier to ground evaluation and evolution.

Method: Define LAF operators and carrier state; operationalize as audit protocol; apply to 161 systems across various carriers; perform representation, temporal, relational, and feed-forward stress tests; analyze decision traces.

Result: Four recurring properties: association does not establish identity; carrier design fixes both the query interface and correction boundary; rendered-view, native-3D, and proposal-level evaluations are not interchangeable; qualifiers such as training-free, real-time, open-vocabulary, and generalizable are meaningful only when attached to a stage and a complete cost ledger.

Conclusion: LAF offers a representation-neutral method for comparing existing systems, diagnosing irreversible failures, and specifying revisable 3D perception for future agents.

Abstract: Methods that transfer predictions from two-dimensional foundation models into three-dimensional segmentation are commonly grouped by task or representation. Those groupings obscure the decisions that determine whether a system remains coherent across views: where image evidence is grounded, when observations become one identity, how semantic and granularity conflicts are handled, which information is fused, and what state survives for later queries. We introduce \textbf{Lift, Associate, and Fuse (LAF)}, a decision-centric framework that represents a transfer system as five operators: \textbf{Generate, Associate, Reconcile, Fuse, and Persist/Query}. LAF defines an explicit contract for the persistent carrier---its spatial support, semantic state, identity state, uncertainty, provenance, and supported operations---and identifies the first stage at which discarded evidence becomes unrecoverable. We operationalize the framework as a structured audit protocol and apply it to 161 systems available through 7 August 2026, spanning point-, field-, Gaussian-, object-, graph-, and memory-based carriers. Representation, temporal, relational, and feed-forward stress tests required no additional analytical stage after the final confirmation pass. The resulting decision traces expose four recurring properties: association does not establish identity; carrier design fixes both the query interface and correction boundary; rendered-view, native-3D, and proposal-level evaluations are not interchangeable; and qualifiers such as \emph{training-free}, \emph{real-time}, \emph{open-vocabulary}, and \emph{generalizable} are meaningful only when attached to a stage and a complete cost ledger. LAF therefore supplies a representation-neutral method for comparing existing systems, diagnosing irreversible failures, and specifying revisable 3D perception for future agents.

</details>


### [14] [Shortcut Learning in a Public Grape Disease Dataset: Annotation Granularity as a Modulator, Not a Cause](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20663)
*Pushuo Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Annotation granularity in public agricultural datasets acts as a modulator of model shortcuts: it can amplify or attenuate cross-species false positives but does not create them; evaluation metrics can mislead when annotation schemes are inconsistent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Public datasets are evaluated by metrics like mAP, but annotation schemes (granularity of labels) may introduce hidden biases that are not captured by reported performance. This paper investigates whether annotation granularity can cause spurious model behavior, particularly cross-species false positives, and evaluates the effect across architectures.

Method: Experimental study on a public grape-disease dataset (3288 images, 11995 boxes, 6 classes) with varying model capacity, input resolution, and detection paradigms. Analysis of cross-species images (5156) with no grape to assess false positives. Counterfactual retraining to test causal impact of granularity on FP, plus placebo controls and pre-registered manipulations in the opposite direction. Introduction of a granularity screening statistic and assessment of airborne lesion-level detection. Comparison across five architectures focusing on small-object bottlenecks.

Result: Test-set mAP50 ranges comparable to seed-to-seed noise, with a bottleneck at small objects across all architectures. One class annotated at whole-leaf level disproportionately contributes to cross-species false positives (65.7% of FPs; 13.41x over its share of training annotations). Counterfactual retraining shows reducing that class’s box granularity lowers cross-species FPs by ~66%; placebo confirms specificity. Coarsening the finest class to whole-leaf level does not induce cross-species FPs for that class and leaves FPs concentrated in the original class. Annotation granularity modulates existing shortcuts but does not create them; destination remains open. A granularity screening statistic is proposed; airborne lesion-level detection is optically out of reach; failure mode is invisible to in-distribution evaluation.

Conclusion: Annotation granularity can amplify or attenuate pre-existing shortcuts in model behavior but is not the root cause. Robust evaluation must account for annotation schemes; the authors provide a granularity screening statistic and highlight limits of in-distribution metrics. Further work is needed to establish how to fix or harmonize annotation granularity to ensure reliable cross-species generalization.

Abstract: Public datasets for agricultural disease detection are usually judged fit for use from reported metrics, which say nothing about whether the annotation scheme is internally consistent. On one public grape disease dataset (3288 images, 11995 boxes, 6 classes), varying model capacity, input resolution and detection paradigm yields a test-set mAP50 range comparable to seed-to-seed noise, with the bottleneck at small objects across all five architectures. The finding lies on the data side: one class is annotated at whole-leaf level (median box area 43.16% of the image) while the other five are annotated at lesion level. On 5156 cross-species images containing no grape, 65.7% of the false-positive boxes fall into that one class, an over-representation of 13.41x relative to its share of the training annotations. Counterfactual retraining establishes a causal effect of granularity on the magnitude of the shortcut: shrinking only that class's boxes cuts its cross-species false positives by 66%, and a placebo control confirms the effect is specific to the manipulated class. A manipulation in the opposite direction, with criteria registered in advance, returns a negative result: coarsening the finest class to whole-leaf level (0.57% to 40.37%), matched in box count and share of annotations and with higher in-distribution AP, still leaves its cross-species false positives at zero boxes, while the unmanipulated original class holds 50.0% of them. Annotation granularity is therefore a modulator of this shortcut, not its cause: it can amplify or attenuate a sink that already exists, but cannot create one, and what fixes the destination remains open. We also give a granularity screening statistic requiring neither images nor training, and show airborne lesion-level detection to be optically out of reach. The failure mode is invisible to in-distribution evaluation.

</details>


### [15] [Aristotelian Manifolds: Leveraging Platonic Perceptual Features for Backpropagation Free Rapid Concept Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20682)
*Michael Karnes,Alper Yilmaz*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A geometry-based framework (Aristotelian Manifolds) analyzes frozen foundation-model representations across domains, revealing non-monotonic semantic maturation and a taxonomy for layer selection and feature compression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Formalize a generalized structural framework for understanding latent spaces in large foundation models, treating them as universal perceptual filters and enabling interpretable, backpropagation-free analyses across architectures and domains.

Method: Layer-wise investigations across diverse architectures and multi-domain datasets; systematic mapping of depth, dimensionality reduction, and distance metrics; profiling peak representational efficiency coordinates to derive a taxonomy for layer choice and feature compression.

Result: Different data domains exhibit distinct geometric response profiles (mound-like peaks in clinical modalities; sigmoidal plateaus in natural vision). Identified exact coordinates of peak efficiency and established a robust, backpropagation-free interpretive framework for latent spaces.

Conclusion: Mapping the internal geometry of frozen representations provides a portable, interpretable framework for understanding and exploiting foundation model latent spaces, guiding layer selection and compression without gradient-based training.

Abstract: This paper formalizes and systematically characterizes Aristotelian Manifolds, a generalized structural framework built upon the Platonic Representation Hypothesis. We position high-capacity foundation models as universal perceptual filters and conduct a comprehensive layer-wise investigation to map how knowledge is functionally synthesized within these latent subspaces. Across diverse architectural paradigms and multi-domain datasets, we rigorously chart the interplay between network depth, dimensionality reduction, and distance metrics. Our characterization reveals that semantic maturation does not follow a singular, monotonic path; instead, different data domains exhibit highly distinct geometric response profiles, characterized by intermediate mound-like peaks for specialized clinical modalities and sigmoidal plateaus for natural visual tasks. By profiling the exact coordinates where these manifolds achieve peak representational efficiency, we establish a predictable taxonomy for layer selection and feature compression. Ultimately, this systematic characterization demonstrates that mapping the internal geometry of frozen representations provides a robust, backpropagation-free, and interpretable framework for understanding and exploiting foundation model latent spaces.

</details>


### [16] [TopoSurfel: Closing the Loop between Gaussian Surfels and Meshes for Surface Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20687)
*Chuanjin Fan,Wenjie Chang,Bohao Liao,Yujia Chen,Wenfei Yang,Tianzhu Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TopoSurfel bridges 3D Gaussian Splatting with continuous meshes via a non-trainable differentiable iso-surfacing step, enabling mesh-guided surfel evolution for robust large-scale reconstruction and high-quality view synthesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address surface reconstruction ambiguities (floaters, holes) in 3D Gaussian Splatting by incorporating an explicit, though differentiable, mesh proxy to guide surfel optimization, improving surface fidelity in textureless/occluded regions.

Method: Extract a continuous proxy mesh using a non-trainable differentiable iso-surfacing process. Use this mesh to guide surfel evolution through normal alignment and geometry-aware density control. Implement a spatially aware hybrid re-initialization strategy for large-scale environments.

Result: Achieves competitive geometric reconstruction accuracy while enabling high-quality mesh-based novel view synthesis; robust against textureless/occluded regions; code available at the provided GitHub link.

Conclusion: TopoSurfel effectively closes the loop between Gaussian surfels and continuous meshes, combining differentiable iso-surfacing with mesh-guided surfel optimization to improve reconstruction robustness and render quality in large-scale scenes.

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting has achieved remarkable success in novel view synthesis. However, extracting high-fidelity surfaces directly from 3DGS remains challenging due to its discrete and unstructured nature. Existing 3DGS-based reconstruction methods typically rely on multi-view geometric consistency or local constraints. Without an explicit structured geometric prior during optimization, these methods often struggle to resolve structural ambiguities, leading to artifacts and floaters, particularly in textureless or occluded regions. To address this limitation, we propose TopoSurfel, a novel framework that closes the loop between Gaussian surfels and continuous meshes. Unlike recent methods that incorporate mesh extraction into the differentiable pipeline by introducing auxiliary neural networks or extra per-Gaussian parameters, we dynamically extract a continuous proxy mesh via a non-trainable differentiable iso-surfacing process. Leveraging this differentiable connection, we introduce a mesh-guided surfel evolution strategy, including normal alignment and geometry-aware density control, to effectively suppress floaters and fill surface holes. Furthermore, to address the initialization challenges in large-scale environments, we propose a spatially aware hybrid re-initialization strategy that ensures robust reconstruction across complex scenes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that TopoSurfel achieves competitive geometric reconstruction accuracy while maintaining high-quality mesh-based novel view synthesis. The code for our method is available at https://github.com/Fan-Treasure/TopoSurfel.

</details>


### [17] [Identity-Aware Human-Object Interaction Motion Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20690)
*Yiming Wang,Yonghao Dang,Huilai Li,Jiawei Tu,Jianqin Yin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes Identity-Aware HOI Motion Captioning. Introduces ID-HOINet with MVIML and TSCR to generate identity-grounded captions from multi-view data, achieving state-of-the-art on BEHAVE and InterCap; code to be released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Limitations in existing HOI motion captioning: captions refer to generic subjects (e.g., "a person"), lacking subject identity grounding. The work aims to ground captions to specific subject identities and leverage multi-view data for robust identity-motion modeling.

Method: ID-HOINet comprises (1) Multi-View Identity-Motion Learning Module (MVIML) that models dependencies across temporal stages and camera viewpoints to capture identity- and motion-related features, learned from multi-view videos; (2) Two-Stage Caption Rewriting Strategy (TSCR) that, during inference, first generates identity-agnostic HOI captions and then rewrites them with predicted identity to produce identity-aware captions.

Result: Experimental results on BEHAVE and InterCap show state-of-the-art performance. Ablations likely demonstrate contributions of MVIML and TSCR. Code will be released upon acceptance.

Conclusion: Identity grounding in HOI motion captioning is achievable via a two-stage framework that leverages multi-view cues to learn identity-aware motion representations and a caption rewriting stage to inject subject identity into the final captions.

Abstract: Existing human-object interaction (HOI) motion captioning methods typically describe what happens while referring to the subject using generic terms such as "a person" or "someone", without grounding the caption in subject identity. To address this limitation, we introduce Identity-Aware Human-Object Interaction Motion Captioning task. This task requires each generated caption to specify both the subject identity and the corresponding HOI motion. For example, the model generates "Sub_ID lifts the chair" rather than "A person lifts the chair". For this task, we design identity-aware HOI motion captions based on the BEHAVE and InterCap datasets. We further propose ID-HOINet, which learns from multi-view videos while supporting single-view identity-aware HOI motion caption generation. ID-HOINet contains two core components: Multi-View Identity-Motion Learning Module (MVIML) and Two-Stage Caption Rewriting Strategy (TSCR). MVIML learns from multi-view videos by modeling dependencies across temporal stages and camera viewpoints, capturing identity and interaction motion features. At inference, the TSCR first retrieves the subject identity and generates identity-agnostic HOI motion captions. TSCR then rewrites these captions with the predicted identity to produce the final identity-aware HOI motion captions. Experiments demonstrate that ID-HOINet achieves state-of-the-art performance. Code will be released upon acceptance.

</details>


### [18] [Bridging Language and Spherical Space: Object-Centric Control for Text-to-Panorama Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20691)
*Derui Li,Qian Qiao,Yuhao Sun,Wenhao Guo,Peng Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes PanoCtrl, an object-centric, controllable text-to-panorama framework that grounds language in spherical space via object-level semantic/positional conditioning, using PanoParse and PanoControl, and introduces PanoGround for evaluation; achieves state-of-the-art spatial alignment and image quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Panoramic generation requires egocentric, spatially-grounded reasoning that existing text-to-panorama methods inadequately address; manual layouts limit language flexibility and misalign with panoramic space.

Method: Introduce PanoParse (text-conditioned parser predicting object semantics and spherical BFoV) and PanoControl (injects object-level semantic/spatial guidance into a diffusion transformer via object-aware attention and spatial residuals). Build PanoGround with object-level spherical annotations and diverse directional descriptions. Evaluate on controllable panorama generation tasks demonstrating improved spatial alignment and image quality.

Result: Extensive experiments show state-of-the-art performance in spatial alignment and image quality for controllable text-to-panorama generation.

Conclusion: Object-centric grounding in spherical space is effective for controllable panorama synthesis; the proposed framework, together with the PanoGround dataset, advances the field toward more faithful and interactive panoramic content creation.

Abstract: Panoramic image generation is increasingly important for immersive applications such as virtual reality, augmented reality, and 3D content creation. Unlike perspective images, panoramic images represent a viewer-centered $360^\circ$ surrounding space, where directional expressions such as left, right, front, and behind play a central role in spatial understanding. However, existing text-to-panorama methods largely rely on implicit spatial reasoning and often fail to faithfully ground object-level directional descriptions in spherical panoramic scenes. A straightforward alternative is to introduce explicit layouts, but requiring manually specified spatial conditions reduces the flexibility of language-based interaction and does not directly resolve the misalignment between egocentric directional language and panoramic image space. To address this issue, we propose PanoCtrl, an object-centric framework for controllable text-to-panorama generation. Our method explicitly bridges natural language and spherical panoramic space by converting textual descriptions into structured object-level spherical conditions and integrating them into the diffusion process. Specifically, we introduce PanoParse, a text-conditioned parser that predicts object semantics and spherical bounding field-of-view (BFoV) parameters, and \textbf{PanoControl}, which injects object-level semantic and spatial guidance into the diffusion transformer through object-aware attention and spatial residual enhancement. To support this task, we construct PanoGround, a dataset with object-level spherical annotations and diverse directional descriptions for controllable panoramic generation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PanoCtrl achieves state-of-the-art performance in both spatial alignment and image quality.

</details>


### [19] [ArtiMo: Agent-Driven Articulated Mesh Animation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20699)
*Chunyu Zou,Peng Dai,Yi-Hua Huang,Ze Yuan,Jingwei Huang,Yeming Yao,Xiaojuan Qi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Zero-shot, agent-driven text-guided articulated mesh animation (ArtiMo) that enforces URDF kinematic constraints via LLM/VLM planning, uses visual self-correction, and introduces a 21-category dataset; improves on complex causal motions without fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Data scarcity for task-specific, causally coherent articulated mesh animation and the need to satisfy strict kinematic constraints during text-driven motion generation; enable zero-shot performance without per-task supervision.

Method: An agent-based pipeline powered by Large Language and Vision-Language Models that plans and generates motions while respecting URDF constraints; a visual self-improvement loop renders animations to keyframes/motion cues for the VLM to diagnose and correct errors; introduces a new 21-category articulated object dataset with causal annotations.

Result: Extensive experiments show ArtiMo significantly outperforms baselines, especially on complex, causally driven motions; strong zero-shot performance and robust handling of causal interactions.

Conclusion: ArtiMo demonstrates that coupling LLM/VLM-based reasoning with explicit kinematic constraints and a self-correcting visual loop enables high-fidelity, causally coherent text-guided animation of articulated meshes without fine-tuning, and provides a valuable benchmark for future work.

Abstract: Animating articulated 3D meshes via text requires satisfying strict kinematic constraints, modeling causal interactions between parts, and achieving instruction fidelity. Due to the absence of task-specific training data and explicit articulation supervision, existing data-driven mesh animation methods are largely inapplicable to this setting. To address this, we propose ArtiMo, a novel agent-driven framework for text-guided articulated mesh animation. Operating in a zero-shot manner, ArtiMo develops an agentic pipeline powered by Large Language and Vision-Language Models (LLMs/VLMs) to orchestrate motion generation. By synergizing the explicit kinematic constraints of URDF with the agent's reasoning and planning capabilities, it effectively produces causally coherent part motions and interactions without requiring model fine-tuning. To ensure motion correctness, the agent additionally utilizes a visual self-improvement mechanism: generated animations are rendered into compact keyframes and motion cues, enabling the VLM to iteratively diagnose and correct errors. Furthermore, we contribute a new benchmark dataset spanning 21 articulated object categories, featuring high-quality motion annotations enriched with causal relationships. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ArtiMo significantly outperforms baselines, particularly on complex, causally driven motions. The project page is available at https://zou-2004.github.io/ArtiMo/.

</details>


### [20] [AGIDefect-4K: A Richly Annotated Dataset for AI-Generated Image Defect Detection, Localization and Explanation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20713)
*Xiangfei Sheng,Weidong Zou,Tianjiao Gu,Zhichao Yang,Pengfei Chen,Leida Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces AGIDefect-4K dataset (4000 images, 15 SOTA generative models) with hierarchical defect annotations and a baseline AGIDA using Multimodal LLMs for joint defect detection, localization, explanation, and quality prediction; benchmarking shows defect understanding remains challenging; dataset publicly released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current AI-generated image (AGI) evaluation benchmarks lack comprehensive defect diagnosis. A fine-grained, multi-level annotation scheme is needed to assess and improve reliability of generative models by identifying, localizing, explaining defects, and quantifying perceptual impact.

Method: Collect 4000 images from 15 diverse generative models (open-source and closed-source). Annotate with: (1) detection labels for defect presence, (2) pixel-level segmentation masks of defective regions, (3) textual explanations describing defect types and perceptual impact, plus an overall quality score per image. Propose AGIDA (AGI Defect Assistant), a baseline framework using Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to perform joint defect detection, localization, explanation, and quality prediction.

Result: Benchmarking on AGIDefect-4K shows that AGI defect understanding remains challenging, underscoring the dataset’s value for advancing evaluation and analysis of defects in synthetic imagery.

Conclusion: AGIDefect-4K provides a rich resource for comprehensive AGI defect diagnosis and benchmarking. AGIDA establishes a baseline for integrated defect understanding via MLLMs, highlighting gaps and guiding future improvements in detection, localization, explanation, and perceptual quality assessment.

Abstract: Generative AI can now produce highly realistic images, yet current models still exhibit subtle but critical defects that undermine their reliability. While existing AI-generated image (AGI) evaluation benchmarks have made notable progress, comprehensive AGI defect diagnosis remains underexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce AGIDefect-4K, a richly annotated dataset of 4,000 images from 15 state-of-the-art generative models spanning both open-source and closed-source systems. AGIDefect-4K features hierarchical defect annotations: (1) detection labels identifying whether defects exist, (2) pixel-level segmentation masks localizing defective regions, and (3) detailed textual explanations characterizing defect types and their perceptual impact. Each image is further annotated with an overall quality score. Building on this, we present AGIDA (AGI Defect Assistant), a baseline framework leveraging Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for joint defect detection, localization, explanation, and quality prediction. Comprehensive benchmarking on AGIDefect-4K reveals that AGI defect understanding remains challenging, underscoring the value of this dataset. The dataset is publicly available at https://github.com/sxfly99/AGIDefect-4K.

</details>


### [21] [AffordAny: Open-World 3D Affordance Grounding from Monocular RGB Images via Vision-Language-Guided Geometric Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20720)
*Junqi Wu,Kaihua Tang,Xuanwen Chen,Hongzhi Li,Jianqiang Huang,Xian-Sheng Hua*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AffordAny enables open-world 3D affordance grounding from a single RGB image by constructing large-scale text-conditioned 3D part supervision, grounding with a frozen vision-language model (VLM) guided decoder, and self-training to expand object coverage. It yields a 5,334-object, 10,633-part dataset across 473 categories and achieves competitive IoU on unseen objects/categories with robustness to instruction paraphrases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D affordance methods rely on pre-built object-centric 3D geometry and closed ontologies, hindering open-world deployment from raw RGB data. A scalable approach is needed to ground functional parts in 3D under free-form language.

Method: From one monocular RGB image, construct text-conditioned 3D part supervision. Use a frozen vision-language model (Cosmos-2B) guided decoder to ground affordances, with the 3D geometry fused via spatial projection, instruction-conditioned semantic compression, and bidirectional geometry-semantics interaction. Introduce minimal-perturbation pseudo-label self-training to add unseen objects and expand coverage. The pipeline produces a benchmark of 5,334 objects and 10,633 part-level samples across 473 categories.

Result: On a systematic generalization protocol, achieved 0.428 IoU on unseen objects and 0.315 IoU on unseen categories after self-training. Unseen-category mIoU improved by 6.3% relative (p<0.01). Instruction sensitivity gap is 0.105, indicating robustness to paraphrase variations.

Conclusion: AffordAny demonstrates effective open-world 3D affordance grounding from monocular input, with a scalable data generation pipeline, a frozen-VLM guided decoder for grounding, and self-training that enhances generalization across unseen objects, categories, and instructions.

Abstract: Open-world 3D affordance grounding requires localizing functional object parts in 3D given free-form language queries. Existing methods typically assume pre-built object-centric 3D geometry and closed affordance ontologies, limiting deployment from raw RGB observations. We present AffordAny, an end-to-end framework that uses one monocular RGB image to construct large-scale text-conditioned 3D part supervision, ground affordances with a frozen vision-language model (VLM) guided decoder, and improve open-world generalization through pseudo-label self-training. Our automated pipeline produces a benchmark of 5,334 objects and 10,633 part-level samples spanning 473 categories, an order-of-magnitude increase in categorical diversity over prior work. The decoder progressively fuses frozen Cosmos-2B features with 3D geometry through spatial projection, instruction-conditioned semantic compression, and bidirectional geometry-semantics interaction. Minimal-perturbation pseudo-label self-training further adds new objects without human annotation. Under a systematic generalization protocol evaluating unseen objects, unseen categories, and unseen instruction paraphrases, our approach achieves 0.428 IoU on unseen objects and 0.315 IoU on unseen categories after self-training, with unseen-category mIoU improving by 6.3% relative (p<0.01) and an instruction sensitivity gap of only 0.105, demonstrating effectiveness and robustness of our method.

</details>


### [22] [VisTa3D: A Dataset and Benchmark for Thin Object Reconstruction from Vision, Tactile, and 3D Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20740)
*Shania Guo,Yeongsik Seo,Andrew Fu,Mei Hao,Iris Xia,Jiwon Jenny Lee,Xinyi Mary Xie,Hyoungseob Park,Aaron Dollar,Alex Wong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: New VisTa3D dataset for thin objects with synchronized RGB, depth, and tactile maps; current 3D reconstruction models struggle with thin objects; introduces a visual-range-tactile baseline; dataset and code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Thin objects occupy little space in RGB and 3D point clouds, making accurate reconstruction difficult; tactile data can provide local shape/deformation cues to supplement visual sensing.

Method: Collected VisTa3D: 387 scenes, 70 thin objects, 17 environments; synchronized RGB, depth, and tactile response maps; includes inertial measurements, camera pose and calibration; ground-truth depth and segmentation maps from laser scanning; benchmarked existing 3D reconstruction models; proposed first visual-range-tactile reconstruction baseline; released code/data.

Result: State-of-the-art 3D reconstruction models show low fidelity on thin objects; the proposed visual-range-tactile baseline demonstrates feasibility of leveraging tactile data to improve reconstruction; dataset enables robust evaluation of multi-modal approaches.

Conclusion: Incorporating tactile information can mitigate thin-object reconstruction challenges; VisTa3D provides a valuable benchmark for developing multi-modal 3D reconstruction methods; future work should quantify gains, explore fusion strategies, and expand object diversity.

Abstract: State-of-the-art 3D reconstruction models, whether from visual, range, or both, tend to underperform on thin objects. This is partially due to the small amount of space such objects occupy in RGB images and in 3D point clouds. To test the extent of their errors, we collected the first thin object dataset comprising of synchronized RGB images, depth maps, and tactile response maps, where each frame is associated with inertial measurements, camera pose and calibration, and groundtruth depth and segmentation maps obtained from laser scanning of thin objects. We hypothesize that tactile data can aid in the reconstruction of thin objects as their response maps provide local shape and deformation information. Our dataset, termed VisTa3D, comprises of 387 scenes covering 70 thin objects over 17 environments. We benchmarked current 3D reconstruction models on VisTa3D and found that, indeed, they exhibit low fidelity on thin objects. To test if tactile data can help, we introduce the first visual-range-tactile 3D reconstruction model as a baseline. Code and data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/shaniaguo/VisTa3D.

</details>


### [23] [Generating Multi-view Adversarial Examples for Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20748)
*Qi Song,Ziyuan Luo,Haoliang Han,Renjie Wan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces MVAP-G, a single-pass multi-view adversarial perturbation generator with cross-view alignment that crafts consistent, imperceptible perturbations across multiple views to attack a 3D reconstruction model (VGGT). It degrades performance without per-scene optimization, highlighting new vulnerabilities in 3D foundation models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Security vulnerabilities in high-performance 3D reconstruction models and the limitations of existing attacks (per-scene optimization, universal perturbations). There is a need to understand and mitigate risks to multi-view/3D vision systems.

Method: A cross-view adversarial alignment mechanism coupled with a feed-forward multi-view perturbation generator (MVAP-G) that produces consistent, imperceptible perturbations across multiple views in a single pass, enabling non-iterative attack during inference.

Result: MVAP-G significantly degrades VGGT performance without requiring iterative optimization at inference time.

Conclusion: Presents the first multi-view adversarial attack on 3D foundation models, revealing substantial vulnerabilities and underscoring the urgency of robust defenses in multi-view 3D vision systems.

Abstract: The Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer (VGGT) enables unified feed-forward 3D reconstruction from multi-view images. However, deploying such a high-performance model may expose critical security vulnerabilities. Traditional adversarial perturbations require costly per-scene optimization, while Universal Adversarial Perturbations (UAPs) rely on a single static pattern and fail to effectively attack VGGT. To address these limitations, we propose \textbf{MVAP-G}, a multi-view adversarial perturbation generator that produces imperceptible consistent perturbations across multiple views in a single feed-forward pass. To ensure perturbation consistency across diverse scenes, we design a cross-view adversarial alignment mechanism to process multi-view images. Experiments demonstrate that MVAP-G significantly degrades VGGT performance without iterative optimization during inference. This work pioneers multi-view adversarial attacks on 3D foundation models, uncovering severe vulnerabilities and underscoring the urgent need for robust 3D vision systems. The code is available at https://github.com/qsong2001/mvap-g.

</details>


### [24] [Multi-Modal Traffic Sign Detection with Semantic Attributes for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20874)
*Meda Lazar,Sourab Sridhar,Shashwata Gupta,Alexandra Tripcea,Varun Ravi,Senthil Yogamani*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A multi-modal framework for robust, long-range, region-agnostic traffic sign perception using camera-LiDAR fusion, non-linear motion modeling, and semantic attribute classification, achieving near-perfect miss ratio on a large, diverse dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Enable globally reliable traffic sign perception essential for commercial-grade autonomous driving, addressing cross-regional generalization, long-range small-object detection, and non-linear perspective-induced temporal instability.

Method: 1) Intensity-Aware Deformable Fusion aligning retro-reflective LiDAR cues with camera features to anchor detection on geometric invariants; 2) a dual motion-model tracker handling non-linear perspective transformations during vehicle approach; 3) semantic attribute classification estimating occlusion, readability, embeddedness, and road relevance.

Result: Extensive evaluation on a dataset spanning 60+ countries and 2,500+ hours of driving data shows an Object Miss Ratio (OMR) of 0.49% over 221,068 evaluation sequences, indicating strong cross-regional generalization and robustness for traffic sign perception.

Conclusion: The pipeline demonstrates globally generalizable traffic sign perception suitable for commercial-grade autonomous driving systems, combining robust multi-modal fusion, temporal tracking, and contextual attributes to improve reliability across regions and conditions.

Abstract: Reliable traffic sign detection is a prerequisite for the global deployment of autonomous driving systems, where regulatory compliance and road safety depend on perceiving signs correctly across regions, ranges, and weather conditions. Despite recent progress, vision-based methods continue to face three fundamental limitations: poor cross-regional generalization due to high diversity across countries, degraded performance on small-object detection at long ranges (traffic signs occupy as little as $10{\times}10$ pixels at 200m), and fragile temporal tracking under the strongly non-linear perspective distortion that occurs as a vehicle approaches a sign. In this paper, we address the problem of robust, long-range, region-agnostic traffic sign perception by combining camera and Light Detection and Ranging (LiDAR) sensing. We present a multi-modal detection framework whose Intensity-Aware Deformable Fusion module aligns retro-reflective LiDAR cues with camera features, anchoring detection on geometric invariants rather than region-specific visual appearance. We further introduce a dual motion-model tracker that explicitly accounts for non-linear perspective transformations during vehicle approach, substantially improving temporal consistency over linear motion assumptions. Additionally, we develop a semantic attribute classification pipeline that estimates occlusion level, readability, sign embeddedness, and road relevance, providing actionable context to downstream planning. Extensive evaluation on our dataset, spanning 60+ countries and 2,500+ hours of driving data, shows that the proposed pipeline achieves an Object Miss Ratio (OMR) of 0.49% across 221,068 evaluation sequences, demonstrating globally generalizable traffic sign perception in commercial-grade autonomous driving systems.

</details>


### [25] [Identity-Preserving Text-to-Video Generation via Agentic Enhancement and Semantic Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20749)
*Jiayi Gao,Changcheng Hua,Jiaqi Tang,Yuxin Peng,Yang Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AESR enhances identity-preserving video generation via an agentic prompt enhancement module and a sample-level visual semantic repair module, with a Mixture-of-Experts selection, achieving state-of-the-art results in ACM MM 2026 Track 1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation:  Addresses identity drift, incomplete instruction following, and missing visual details in commercial video-generation models. Raises issues due to closed-source models that prevent direct parameter optimization, necessitating a lightweight, post-hoc enhancement framework.

Method:  Global agentic prompt enhancement module: learns model-specific prompting formats from official documentation, leverages human-interaction priors, builds a reusable playbook via an agentic loop. Sample-level visual semantic repair: uses a vision-language model to locate erroneous segments, designs repair instructions, edits frames to explicit references, guides a video-editing model for local fixes. Lightweight Mixture-of-Experts to select reliable outputs across generation and refinement paths.

Result: Achieved first place in Track 1 of the ACM MM 2026 Identity-Preserving Video Generation Challenge (MIPL_Video). Code released at the provided GitHub link.

Conclusion: AESR provides a practical, model-agnostic enhancement pipeline that improves prompt quality and local semantic fidelity without modifying base generation models, demonstrating strong performance and potential for real-world identity-preserving video tasks.
